#### Interpretation and alternatives

**CL-B0251-001 — Hospitality and stage authority coexist**

The tofu opening lets Hitoha and Futaba enter as interested guests before Musubi can stabilize them as intruders. Hime's reflexive food offer and Sawara's audience-based welcome coexist with Musubi's explicit objection to unilateral stage action. Momoka's commercial and labor-saving motives make the welcome heterogeneous. The chapter supports an opening toward contact, while the procedure and authority for the coming live remain unsettled.

**CL-B0251-002 — Recognition preserves a different artistic possibility**

Musubi initially describes KARAKURI through an intimidating vertical comparison. Hitoha and Futaba sustain their own perfection/optimization rhetoric, yet their agreement with Musubi's claim to a unique stage admits a second criterion of value. Hitoha identifies meaningful seeking; Futaba names a possibility different from their own. The literary turn lies in recognition across distinct performance approaches, not in the analyst verifying their technical superlatives or assigning a winner.

**CL-B0251-003 — Different-colored partnership as an attributed interpretation**

The twins read Musubi's strain first as fan-directed passion and then as effort for Susu. Hitoha's second explanation is a question; Futaba's interest is her own appraisal. Musubi partly concedes the fan-directed account and disclaims sole credit, but she resists the final kindness label and asks the twins to stop. Existing NI+CORA history makes relational labor a useful comparison, without turning the twins' proposed motives into complete access to Musubi's private intention or Susu's consent.

**CL-B0251-004 — Praise does not have a single shared emotional result**

Musubi's fluster, requests to stop and exit keep the scene from becoming a simple successful lesson in receiving praise. Hime reads the difficulty as how to praise her, Momoka enjoys the changing expressions and Sawara reports the departure. Musubi's later apology confirms that she left and calmed herself; it does not retrospectively make her embarrassment enjoyable to her or endorse every evaluation spoken during it.

**CL-B0251-005 — Fandom changes the relevance of artistic prestige**

Momoka initially insulates herself by declaring serious artists outside her interests. The twins connect their work to a franchise she already values; ending-theme and insert-song reports make their brand newly relevant within her own field. Her enthusiastic assessment, honorific address and ticket/autograph requests articulate the shift. Hime first imagines a plot and later intimidation, whereas Sawara and eventually Hime recognize a coherent fan response. The chapter's reversal therefore distinguishes the twins' predicted unavoidable impact, Momoka's own declared interest and her observers' changing explanations.

#### Interpretation and alternatives

**CL-B0251-010 — The challengers encounter a return visit they do not fully control**

The opening preserves the twins’ confidence in their successful provocation, but the paired qualifications make that confidence incomplete before Sawara arrives. Nanasta then takes the backstage initiative and reveals additional members whom the twins’ ordinary intruder script has not anticipated. The concluding storm comparison relocates surprise onto KARAKURI’s side. This is a local reversal of initiative, not proof that their professional capacity has been defeated or that the visitors had a single coordinated plan.

Sawara’s report of the Player’s gratitude changes the meaning available to the twins: a disruptive appearance can also be a welcome learning opportunity for 777. Hitoha’s pleasure and Futaba’s conditional sense of reward make his enjoyment matter without making him physically present or converting Sawara’s account into a shown contract. **CL-B0251-012 — Reciprocal personhood resists a quick explanation**

The twins answer a question about special people by naming one another in separate first-person statements. Their attachment is reciprocal, not an interchangeable collective voice. Sawara offers only partial understanding, and Futaba immediately polices the boundary against instant comprehension. The scene licenses an interpretation of closeness alongside guarded access; it does not license a clinical diagnosis, a complete causal theory of their teasing, or a permanent exclusion of everyone else.

**CL-B0251-013 — Musubi supplies a motive after the earlier encounter**

Musubi’s purpose is to thank the visitors for relieving her self-imposed pressure. Her first-person acknowledgment provides new evidence beyond the twins’ previous teasing interpretation. At the same time, she asks them to stop the label through which they package that kindness. Recognition helps her speak without requiring acceptance of every term imposed by the recognizers. **CL-B0251-014 — Different partnerships can be valued without declaring equal technique**

Musubi accepts a current asymmetry in unison while insisting on the particular bonds of NI+CORA and 777. The tofu-and-condiment exchange subsequently offers a material analogy for value generated through difference and combination. The analogy supports the family’s concern with an individually meaningful stage; it is not an objective musical assessment or a demonstration that one duo has already equaled another. Musubi’s tofu qualification explicitly restrains Hime’s attempt to transfer culinary praise directly onto stage status.

**CL-B0251-015 — Rivalry becomes a request for further contact**

Musubi’s thanks and request for later performance impressions transform a rival’s visit into a relationship she actively wants to continue. Her statements of nerves, rivalry and special bonds remain alongside that welcome. Hime’s future challenge likewise preserves competition. The resulting openness is not submission, retirement of rivalry, or evidence that the future live has already produced the hoped-for learning.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0206.json__9f071cf7e585168e?doc=primary&log=39#/Pages/39` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0206.json__9f071cf7e585168e?doc=primary&log=45#/Pages/45` (complete P39-45 context); `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0206.json__9f071cf7e585168e?doc=primary&log=57#/Pages/57` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0206.json__9f071cf7e585168e?doc=primary&log=61#/Pages/61` (complete P57-61 context); `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0206.json__9f071cf7e585168e?doc=primary&log=79#/Pages/79`; `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0206.json__9f071cf7e585168e?doc=primary&log=80#/Pages/80`.

**CL-B0251-016 — Care has named agents and a local invitation**

Sawara first initiates contact, Hime observes, Momoka participates in the two-person continuation, and Futaba then specifically invites Musubi. Keeping these roles separate prevents a generic account in which every Nanasta member pats an undifferentiated KARAKURI. The explicit invitation is strong local evidence for Musubi’s participation, but it is not a retrospective blanket consent for the initial pat or for unrelated future contact. Hitoha’s final denial also keeps the organizers’ playful framing contestable.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0206.json__9f071cf7e585168e?doc=primary&log=62#/Pages/62` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0206.json__9f071cf7e585168e?doc=primary&log=78#/Pages/78` (complete P62-78 context).

**CL-B0251-017 — P74 preserves an unresolved attribution problem**

The source’s Hitoha Name field and its address to Hitoha pull in different directions. The repeated joint sprite and unheard cue cannot adjudicate that conflict. The safest account retains the literal label, identifies the address, and leaves the intended speaker uncertain. Moreover the line is a teasing question about Susu, not evidence that a Susu head-patting history has independently been established. No silent reassignment to Futaba or source repair is justified.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0206.json__9f071cf7e585168e?doc=primary&log=73#/Pages/73` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0206.json__9f071cf7e585168e?doc=primary&log=76#/Pages/76` (complete P73-76 context).

**CL-B0251-018 — Filming is a privacy issue within the exchange, not an admitted new source**

Momoka’s claim of secret filming explains her objection to being revealed, while Musubi’s deletion demand marks a limit on that behavior. Musubi’s legal adjective belongs to her dialogue. The scene neither shows deletion nor provides the alleged footage for inspection, and the analytical program does not admit hypothetical footage from this remark. The participants’ hidden presence also means their precise earliest access to the opening conversation cannot be inferred merely from sprite absence.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0206.json__9f071cf7e585168e?doc=primary&log=31#/Pages/31` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0206.json__9f071cf7e585168e?doc=primary&log=34#/Pages/34` (complete P31-34 context).

**CL-B0251-019 — Gifts change the close of an intrusion**

Hime’s tofu and Momoka’s snack are named contributions that remain after the Nanasta members leave. They let the closing twins discuss taste and make reciprocal feeding offers rather than continue a purely adversarial assessment of weaknesses. This is an evidenced shift in local interaction. It does not establish a permanent personality conversion, a fully observed eating routine, the completion of autograph or ticket promises, or the result of the forthcoming live.

**CL-B0251-020 — The reader gains more than every participant necessarily knows**

The reader can join Sawara’s report, the twins’ private reciprocal answers, Musubi’s later self-account and the duo’s final exchange. The text does not give every participant that total view. Hidden visitors’ precise arrival and hearing are unresolved; Susu and the Player are referenced without appearing; the departing visitors are not shown receiving the final snack exchange. Analytical synthesis must retain these unequal access boundaries.

**CL-B0251-021 — Static unity and native staging remain bounded evidence**

The shared two-body sprite provides a consistent visual emblem of coordination while separate labels and reciprocal propositions preserve two people. Native effects, slot fades, speaker flags and timing numbers supply authored presentation instructions. They do not turn the 95 available but unheard WAVs into performed evidence or let the unobserved runtime confirm exact overlap, silence, patting movement, feeding or concert execution.

#### Source-local character and collective changes

- **Hitoha / CHAR-0025:** Hitoha welcomes the reported Player enjoyment, names Futaba as personally special, appreciates complementary taste, separately describes the contact she receives, contests Sawara’s meeting frame and initiates a snack-feeding offer. #### Native presentation and actually reviewed stills

- All 17 ordered parts (text_001–009 and native_001–008) were actually acquired; the preserved lossless native reconstruction equals the bound 97-page packet. No future chapter primary was opened for this case.
- Command-only P0, P84 and P96 coexist with 94 written dialogue pages. Background bg_079 is selected throughout; P0 and P96 have AutoMove 40, the other 95 pages AutoMove 0. These are instructions rather than observed elapsed times.
- Native white flashes at P0/P96, yellow flash P6 and shake effects P64/P77 are reviewed as metadata. All IsTremble values are false; these facts do not exclude an effect shake or establish performed movement.
- The selected character resource set is 31 sprites, no Items, one background and ten blank non-asset markers. P1–95 have seven-slot character arrays with null outer slots; front/speaker flags and varying active slots were reviewed without treating blank markers as missing assets.
- P84 assigns visitor fades with native values 1, approximately 0.333333343 and 0.5 for the visible departing slots. No watched runtime or exact acted interval is inferred.
- The current title-image inventory is empty; native SubTitle contains 後編 at P0 and 終わり at P96. No Choices, InlineMovie, Loop or Item fields are present in this current complete native inventory.
- Available unheard BGM references include bgm_005,006,023,008. All 95 WAVs are metadata-bound and UNHEARD; no performed acoustic claim follows from the voice sequence or carrier.
- P74 exact native TextArea, Sound and Charas were independently retained in source_p74_label_addressee_review_v1.json. Literal Name is 空栗ヒトハ; its dialogue addresses ヒトハ; cue176 is unheard and a joint143 sprite cannot resolve intended speaker.
- Actual post-truncation image recovery reviewed the contact, all 31 labeled composites, the full bg_079 image and an enlarged143_01_04. The prior truncated result remains preserved and uncredited.

- Actual fresh recovery acquisition: the complete 1440x3360 current-case contact sheet (cc86783ae460b3a43d5230024aea92c7b64649547fc9a1d8e608baeeae903650) was delivered alone and reviewed without truncation; bg_079 was then delivered alone and reviewed. The earlier combined result was truncated and receives no view credit; the separate recovery note preserves that failure.
- All 31 selected sprite composites were actually seen on that contact: nine Musubi 004 school-uniform facial variants, eight Hime 006 sailor-uniform variants, five Momoka 007 decorated pink-jacket variants, four Sawara 012 blue-apron variants, and five joint 143 variants. These are fixed still resources with changing mouths, brows and eyes; they do not establish performed tone, exact motion or acted timing.
- The additional 143_01_04 composite was independently enlarged and viewed. It depicts two pink-haired bodies in matching light uniforms with linked/crossing arms and distinct faces, both mouths open in this variant. The five shared assets support a coordinated presentation without identifying a permanent left/right name assignment, fusing the two people, or resolving P74’s label/addressee conflict.
- Musubi’s fixed uniform pose and Hime’s hands-at-hips pose persist across expressions. Momoka’s jacket decorations and Sawara’s apron are visible. No composite depicts a multi-person head-patting choreography; that action is supported by the written exchange and native page staging, not by inventing an unseen animation from these stills.
- The actual bg_079 image is an unoccupied, brightly lit dressing-room interior with changing space, clothing rail, vanity mirrors and lamps, chairs, a table, cups, bottled drinks and generic refreshments. It supports the backstage setting. Its generic food art does not independently identify Hime’s tofu, Momoka’s named snack, an actual transfer, consumption sequence, date or who was hidden where.
- There are no current Item resources. Ten blank non-asset character markers are native staging values, not missing sprites to fabricate. All 31 sprites and the single background are credited as fresh current-case actual views; no additional earlier-view reuse is needed for this static inventory.

### Complete-family factual reconstruction

- The first chapter begins around Nanasta’s preparations for an upcoming live. Hime reports an absent Player’s conditional permission for a break if Coney agrees and brings premium tofu from her family shop for the event’s special menu. Hitoha and Futaba visit, initially interested in the food and rehearsals, while Musubi questions their authority to intrude on a 777 stage. Sawara welcomes them as a fan, Hime responds with excitement, and Momoka approaches through her own commercial and leisure interests. These are different responses by named members, rather than one unanimous host attitude. [507020501 P2–25]
- Musubi recalls an imposing earlier KARAKURI performance with little MC and notes the duo’s awareness of NI+CORA. Hitoha and Futaba make their own claims about perfectly coordinated, economical unison and probe Musubi’s greater tension beside Susu. Musubi proposes that a stage with meaning unique to its performers can exist beyond that model. The twins treat the difference between partners as an interesting possibility; Musubi rejects their so-kind framing and leaves the exchange. The first chapter freezes before any later self-explanation can settle her motive. [507020501 P26–59]
- Momoka initially disclaims interest in serious performers. Her response changes after the twins discuss her favorite SF anime, トーキョーＵＧウォー, and report their coming ending-theme and insert-song involvement in its announced film. Their emotional-musical predictions remain claims about a future film, not a shown performance. Momoka’s excitement and her request to address the duo as KARAKURI-san concern this specific fan interest. Hime cites her younger siblings’ enthusiasm; Sawara reports Shinju’s wish to sing an ending theme. No film, booking for Shinju, ticket delivery or completed autograph is shown. Musubi returns after calming down and misses the intervening explanation for Momoka’s enthusiasm. [507020501 P61–103]
- In the second chapter, the twins reflect that they disturbed 777’s pace but qualify their confidence. Sawara enters their dressing room in a reciprocal intrusion and personally welcomes their upcoming live participation. She reports the Player’s appreciation of the chance for 777 to see KARAKURI nearby. Hitoha and Futaba value his enjoyment in separate formulations; when Sawara asks who is special to them personally, each names the other. Sawara claims a little understanding of their teasing, Futaba resists being understood from a short conversation, and Sawara disclaims the stronger reading. [507020502 P1–30]
- Musubi, Hime and Momoka are revealed as additional visitors. Hime wanted them treated as absent, Momoka claims secret filming, and Musubi demands deletion of what Musubi calls illegal data. The scene does not show deletion or establish the precise beginning of their hidden presence and hearing. Musubi then distinguishes her purpose from an unplanned visit: she thanks the twins, says their earlier exchange loosened her pressure, acknowledges her wish to make a good stage with Susu, and asserts particular NI+CORA and 777 bonds while currently appraising KARAKURI’s unison as superior. She still asks them to stop the so-kind wording. [507020502 P31–45]
- Momoka says sharing a stage with KARAKURI and possibly obtaining an autograph motivates her. Hime offers the tofu as a return gift and extends her family’s unique taste into a claim for an unreproducible stage. Hitoha praises the mutual enhancement of condiments and tofu; Futaba praises the soy ingredient’s realized value and uniqueness. Musubi reminds Hime that the immediate praise is for tofu. Recognition and reciprocity emerge without converting that culinary exchange into a demonstrated musical result. Musubi remains a rival and Hime predicts a future challenge. [507020502 P46–61]
- Sawara initiates double head patting; Hitoha and Futaba separately report it, Hime observes, and Momoka joins the continuation supported by her blessings joke and Hitoha’s report of two patters. Futaba specifically invites Musubi, who accepts briefly. P74 literally labels Hitoha but addresses Hitoha in a question about patting Susu; intended individual attribution remains unresolved. Sawara names and ends the playful patting meeting while Hitoha denies that such a meeting was convened. Sawara renews a shared-live invitation, Musubi asks for later performance impressions, and Hime and Momoka leave their respective foods. [507020502 P62–84]
- The closing private exchange reverses the twins’ opening confidence: Hitoha describes Nanasta as arriving and departing like a storm, while Futaba acknowledges more than easy susceptibility to provocation. Hitoha offers to feed Futaba the snack and Futaba offers reciprocally; each comments on sweetness before their joint anticipation of the live. The script supplies offers and flavor comments, without showing a full feeding choreography. The two-chapter family ends before the actual concert, feedback, ticket or autograph outcomes. [507020502 P85–96]

### Complete-family causal structure

- Local continuity is explicit rather than inferred solely from adjacent catalog rows. The first chapter’s announced forthcoming stage, Musubi’s flight and return, the second chapter’s reference to the earlier exchange, continuing voice sequence and front/back subtitles connect the two chapters. They do not establish an exact calendar date or a total ordering among other Event families.
- The twins’ performance confidence makes individual host reactions useful provocations, but the hosts are not interchangeable targets. Musubi is reached through recognition of her effort and partner difference, Momoka through a particular anime interest, and Hime through family food and the claim to a unique stage. Sawara’s welcome provides a further route that neither reduces the twins to threat nor treats their arrival as a technical victory for Nanasta.
- The first chapter leaves competing interpretations of Musubi’s response open. The second adds Musubi’s own deliberate thank-you and account of reduced pressure, licensing a forward update. It does not make the twins’ earlier guesses retrospectively objective, prove a universal therapeutic effect, or remove Musubi’s stated boundary against their repeated label.
- The visitors’ return intrusion redistributes initiative. Sawara’s questions, the hidden visitors’ reveal, food gifts and specific physical contact make KARAKURI the recipients of unpredictable attention. The final storm comparison makes that reversal explicit at the level of the twins’ own description; it does not establish a defeated musical ranking or a single secret plan shared by every visitor.
- The family develops complementarity through successive scales: Musubi’s particular bonds with Susu and 777, the twins’ separately spoken reciprocal attachment, and the tofu/condiment appraisal. These are source-backed juxtapositions that make difference relevant to value. Culinary harmony is an interpretive analogy, not an empirical proof of an unperformed stage or permanent equivalence among units.
- Thanks, recognized effort and gifts turn an intrusion into further desired contact: Musubi now asks the duo to stay and comment, Sawara renews the welcome, and the twins close with real anticipation. Rivalry, teasing, resistance to quick understanding, a deletion demand and a contested meeting label remain in the same resolution. The result is local negotiated openness rather than complete harmony or a finished personality transformation.

### Complete-family epistemic boundaries

- The whole-family reader can connect both chapters, but the first chapter’s frozen known-then record stays unchanged. Musubi’s later self-account is not treated as information already available during her earlier exit.
- Musubi is not present for the intervening anime-credit discussion after her exit; her late confusion and Sawara’s minimal explanation preserve that gap. The reader’s knowledge of Momoka’s particular fan response does not automatically become Musubi’s knowledge.
- The Player and Coney are absent and reached through Hime’s or Sawara’s reports. Susu, Shinju, Hime’s siblings and the earlier 4U comparison are references, not additional present speakers. A report, recollection, aspiration or hypothetical is not a witnessed new scene.
- Hitoha’s and Futaba’s personal answers are separate propositions. Sawara’s little understanding remains her tentative interpretation, explicitly challenged by Futaba. Neither that interpretation nor the reader’s prior archive establishes complete access to either twin’s interior life or exact motives.
- Musubi, Hime and Momoka are revealed as hidden visitors in the second chapter, but their earliest arrival and hearing are not fixed by first sprite appearance. The analysis does not assign them the entire preceding conversation or prove their earlier ignorance.
- Momoka’s filming claim is not inspected footage. Musubi’s deletion order and legal characterization are dialogue; actual deletion and external legality remain unverified. No hypothetical covert video is admitted to the source corpus.
- P74 retains the literal Hitoha label and address to Hitoha as an unresolved source attribution conflict. The unheard cue and shared two-body sprite cannot decide intended speaker. The Susu content is a teasing question rather than confirmation of a past head-patting routine.
- The final snack exchange occurs after the visitors’ departure staging. No absent visitor is shown receiving those closing remarks. Offers to feed, separate flavor comments and written laughter remain distinct from a watched full eating routine or performed sound.

### Complete-family character relations

- Hitoha and Futaba remain two separately routed people. Their matching presentation and joint lines coexist with different first-person diction, reciprocal naming, separate valuations and separately voiced taste comments. Their closeness does not erase individual subjecthood or impose a permanent leader/follower division.
- KARAKURI and Musubi move from an authority challenge and uncomfortable praise toward a deliberate thank-you and desired future feedback. Musubi can acknowledge relative unison, value unique bonds, accept a particular invitation and retain resistance to the duo’s label at the same time.
- Musubi’s account of Susu and NI+CORA is hers to give; Susu does not appear to confirm it. The reviewed NI+CORA priors preserve privacy, negotiated difference and care reversals without using this chapter to prove a generalized consent, domestic routine or exhaustive duo motive.
- Sawara provides welcome, reported Player approval, questioning and a playful older-sister role. She initiates the first head pat and later renews the live invitation, but she neither fully diagnoses the twins nor speaks with demonstrated consent from all participants for every action.
- Hime contributes the family tofu, interprets its uniqueness as a stage analogy, feels recognized and maintains a future competitive challenge. Musubi’s tofu qualification remains essential. Hime is an observer of the first head pat and a named donor, not an invented patting agent.
- Momoka’s specific fan interests explain her excitement and prospective autograph request. She also claims filming, participates in the patting continuation, teases Musubi and leaves the snack. The family does not reduce her to generic laziness, establish completed media work, or certify a permanent motivation change.
- The head-patting sequence has distinct agents, observers, recipients and an explicit later invitation: Sawara initiates; Hime observes; Momoka joins; Futaba invites Musubi; Musubi accepts briefly; Hitoha/Futaba react separately. A local invitation is not blanket or retroactive consent. Hitoha’s denial of a formally convened meeting preserves a boundary inside the joke.
- The gifts remain separately attributed: Hime’s tofu and Momoka’s チューバックス. The twins’ closing reciprocal offers and shared anticipation register a local response to those contributions without giving the departed donors access to that private ending.

### Complete-family literary synthesis and alternatives

**CL-B0251-022 — Recognition permits a different performance rather than supplying one ideal**

Hitoha and Futaba claim economical perfect unison yet also identify Musubi's partner-directed work as a different possibility. Musubi initially resists their praise and later explains how it relieved pressure to be their equal. Their recognition matters because it preserves an asymmetry rather than certifying equal skill. The alternative—that she simply becomes a compliant fan—fails to account for her retained stage-authority, label and ranking objections.

**CL-B0251-023 — A change of host redistributes the power to unsettle**

The twins' planned intrusion gives way to Sawara's return visit, hidden listeners' revelation and the visitors' gifts. This reversal makes KARAKURI recipients of attention without abolishing privacy or interpretive limits: Musubi demands deletion, Futaba resists Sawara's quick understanding, and the earliest hidden hearing remains unknown. Hospitality and welcome coexist with those boundaries.

**CL-B0251-024 — The special other is named reciprocally without fusing the twins**

Hitoha names Futaba and Futaba names Hitoha. Their separate valuations and closing feeding offers make the reciprocal bond legible while the source supplies neither a single merged psychology nor a fixed left/right assignment from shared art. Sawara's partial understanding is itself qualified by Futaba; intimacy is not an analyst's entitlement to total explanation.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0206.json__9f071cf7e585168e?doc=primary&log=13#/Pages/13` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0206.json__9f071cf7e585168e?doc=primary&log=30#/Pages/30` (complete P13-30 context); `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0206.json__9f071cf7e585168e?doc=primary&log=85#/Pages/85` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0206.json__9f071cf7e585168e?doc=primary&log=95#/Pages/95` (complete P85-95 context).

**CL-B0251-025 — Fan taste and family goods complicate a single prestige hierarchy**

Momoka's response changes when the twins' reported film work enters her own franchise interest, while Hime connects tofu's specificity to a stage only its makers can produce. The first is a coherent preference, the second an analogy that Musubi immediately qualifies as praise for tofu. Neither proves general submission, completed ticket/autograph transfer or an objective equivalence between cuisine and performance.

#### Interpretation and alternatives

**CL-B0251-026 — Post-live recognition remains distributed testimony**

The celebration lets performers hear how particular others describe their work. Rona, Rena, the Player, Hina, Sawara, Honoka and others supply different vocabularies rather than a common judging scale. The completed-live frame is explicit, but the analyzer has only their written accounts and the party’s native/still presentation. Keeping that distinction allows a real local achievement and its social reception to be analyzed without manufacturing a performed concert review.

**CL-B0251-027 — Musubi permits a limited claim to her own accomplishment**

Musubi moves from relief into the cautious proposition that she may praise herself a little. Haru amplifies it into a joking public acclamation, and Musubi objects without retracting the initial allowance. The earlier KARAKURI family provides a separately frozen account of Musubi discussing pressure and particular bonds; the two scenes can be compared as situated self-appraisals without attributing every later success to one conversation or forcing a complete calendar from adjacency.

**CL-B0251-028 — Recognition can include a correction of the recognizer**

The praise does not require performers to accept every frame offered to them. Ume objects first to manly and later to reducing the band to food metaphors, while Coney stresses that praise was intended. Shinju can be grateful and embarrassed by Hina’s description at once. These small objections retain the recipients’ standpoint inside a convivial scene. Well-meant recognition is an ongoing negotiation, not proof that every description or teasing relation is welcomed universally.

**CL-B0251-029 — Competitive confidence becomes a visible relational rhyme**

Ume’s confident formula is repeated by Kyoko, then made an explicit joke by Musubi and Momoka and answered under a compound Kyoko/Ume label. The structure invites comparison between performers from different units, while their names, images and immediate reactions keep them separate. Rena’s coordinated reading of 4U and Honoka’s aspiration add an interpersonal benchmark that the Player redirects toward competitive ambition. None of these lines proves identical personalities, objective equality of skill or a new merged unit.

#### Questions and limits at this original exit

- What full program, setlist, stage action and audience response lie behind these different party appraisals? The present evidence supplies attributed accounts rather than a performed recording.

- How will this source-local post-live feedback relate to the bounded Haru/Kajika collaboration’s continuation in other undated frames? Current praise alone does not settle permanent status or exact chronology.
- How does Honoka receive the dialect affirmation and attention scene beyond these immediate lines? No durable resolution or romance is established.
- What further aftermath remains beyond this first chapter’s native fade? The next primary stays unopened until this case is genuinely frozen.

- Honoka is the reported backstage monitor watcher; Momoka, Rona and Rena contribute different reports. The near-spill is not an actual confirmed spill, and the source does not provide the monitor recording or incident footage.

#### Interpretation and alternatives

**CL-B0251-038 — Recognition is tested against different ways of perceiving**

Sumire’s difficulty reading enjoyment from unchanging faces is not the same as proof that the twins felt none. Sui and Sawara describe movement rather than facial display; Hitoha’s and Futaba’s later first-person accounts give their perceptions local support. Emoco dismisses the informal vocabulary, but the duo themselves find something perceptive in it. The scene distinguishes intelligibility from polished explanatory language without making any observer infallible or supplying an objective technical ranking.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=16#/Pages/16` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=30#/Pages/30` (complete P16-30 context).

**CL-B0251-039 — The twins describe audience participation inside their own precision model**

Hitoha retains the language of prediction and synchronization, yet reports a result beyond prediction. Futaba locates surprise in audience heat and the hearts enjoying it. Their coordinated performance is thus described by its participants as responsive to a social encounter rather than fully exhausted by an internal calculation. This develops their source-local self-account; it does not independently measure a sync rate, merge their minds or prove a permanent transformation of their entire professional practice.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=25#/Pages/25` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=28#/Pages/28` (complete P25-28 context).

**CL-B0251-040 — Unclassifiable care unsettles a confident observer role**

The twins classify Shinju and Kajika but stop short at Sawara, who has already intruded on their terms in the earlier frozen family. Shizuka celebrates recognition while the Player and Shinju question its form. The later credit given to Sawara’s perception adds another direction to observation: someone the duo cannot neatly classify can read something meaningful in them. The scene preserves teasing and uncertainty rather than turning those labels into exhaustive role assignments.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=4#/Pages/4` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=15#/Pages/15` (complete P4-15 context); `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=27#/Pages/27`; `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=28#/Pages/28`.

**CL-B0251-041 — Sumire demands particularity from managerial praise**

A generic positive response is insufficient for Sumire because it could be given to anyone. Her objection draws the Player and other listeners into more particular descriptions of SiSH. Sui’s unchanged self-feeling then complicates the difference others perceive. The result is a discussion of situated presentation, not a discovery that the members are false in daily life or that their public older-sister image exhausts who they are.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=34#/Pages/34` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=44#/Pages/44` (complete P34-44 context).

**CL-B0251-042 — Rena offers a plural account of self-knowledge, which Coney amplifies**

Rena first proposes that an audience may find an aspect of Sui she does not know. Coney names Rena as the source before expanding the idea to fans and the Player, and Sumire relates it to her own history. Other people’s recognition can reveal possibilities without invalidating the performer’s own unchanged experience. The manager-centered amplification remains one interpretation; it does not grant fans or management final authority over identity, choices, touch or every private motive.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=43#/Pages/43` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=50#/Pages/50` (complete P43-50 context).

**CL-B0251-043 — The discovery theme is immediately exposed to an unstable double meaning**

Sui’s made-me-a-woman phrasing moves the discussion from being seen differently into a misunderstanding. Honoka’s shock, Rona’s warning, the compound accusation, the Player’s denial/cry and Emoco’s deflection are different responses. The joke shows how a benevolent recognition story can be rerouted through the manager’s disputed intimacy role. It does not establish sexual history, a gender-state change, exact bodily punishment or consent; those would require evidence this exchange does not provide.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=50#/Pages/50` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=58#/Pages/58` (complete P50-58 context).

**CL-B0251-044 — Family work supplies the celebration’s material underside**

Emoco’s complaint about catering is answered by Shinju’s named Uoharu sashimi and Hime’s family tofu/okara. These ordinary-work networks make a concrete contribution beside the language of performance discovery. The later exhausted budget and request that the Player pay keep resource limits present in the ending. The source does not provide finances or a complete supplier/staff census, and the warm collective resolution does not itself solve older household labor or manager-workload problems.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=58#/Pages/58`; `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=59#/Pages/59`; `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=60#/Pages/60`; `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=98#/Pages/98` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=101#/Pages/101` (complete P98-101 context).

**CL-B0251-045 — The closing speaker admits limited understanding rather than supplying a total official account**

After being pressed to speak, the Player describes work so busy that the achievement still feels difficult to grasp. Hime’s and Kyoko’s criticism preserves an expectation of composure and managerial awareness; his apology does not erase it. He then limits his certainty to wanting to make another live with the others. This is a situated desire and request, not a complete account of the project, a clinical memory diagnosis, proof of sustainable labor or a claim that institutional responsibility no longer matters.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=63#/Pages/63` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=80#/Pages/80` (complete P63-80 context).

**CL-B0251-046 — Renewed cooperation has different speakers and degrees of assent**

The affirmative responses have different objects and idioms: Haru’s direct agreement, Susu’s devotion, Sawara’s acceptance, Hime’s festival wish and Sui’s anticipation. Kyoko speaks for a trio without separate confirmation from both others.4U receives a separate invitation and Ume offers consideration, while KARAKURI retains an intrusion idiom. The scene can renew a field of possible cooperation without becoming a unanimous contract, permanent combined lineup or proof that the next show has occurred.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=81#/Pages/81` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=94#/Pages/94` (complete P81-94 context).

**CL-B0251-047 — Rivalry survives inside the invitation to return**

The Player does not absorb 4U and KARAKURI into Nanasta by asking them back. Ume’s qualified answer, Emoco’s barb and Hina’s affectionate reading preserve the band’s internal exchange, while Hitoha and Futaba make their own confident proposal. Distinct institutions and different voices remain inside a shared possibility. Hina’s closeness judgment does not negate Ume’s protest or tell us the group’s entire future decision.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=87#/Pages/87` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=94#/Pages/94` (complete P87-94 context).

**CL-B0251-048 — The budget punchline limits the celebratory future**

Coney’s forecast and second-party proposal briefly make continuation seem automatic. The Player’s budget objection interrupts that ease; the request that he pay converts the institutional limit into a personal demand, which he protests as familiar. The comedy can express group intimacy while retaining an unresolved cost and consent problem. No actual payment or universal individual assent should be added merely because a collective label answers.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=95#/Pages/95` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=104#/Pages/104` (complete P95-104 context).

**CL-B0251-049 — A large collective promise ends with a small unfinished personal connection**

After the collective joke, the focus narrows to Ume’s hesitant return to a pre-live promise and Haru’s eager recollection. The current agreement advances the previously published open request but does not display an address or completed correspondence. It makes renewed connection specific to two named people rather than only a mass future live. The coda’s two sprites do not by themselves prove privacy, and its expression variants do not establish a romance or a performed emotional trajectory.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=105#/Pages/105` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0208.json__28adabcecdc5dfee?doc=primary&log=112#/Pages/112` (complete P105-112 context).

**CL-B0251-050 — Native presentation marks turns without supplying a watched event**

Black slides separate party vignettes, the BGM reference changes to028 with Haru’s affirmative response, and white transitions frame the budget close and Haru/Ume coda. These are authored structural instructions that can be located precisely. They are not heard musical transitions or observed durations; the exceptional AutoMove20 on Haru’s written P105 also prevents a false blanket claim that every dialogue page waits identically.

#### Interpretation and alternatives

**CL-B0251-055 — Rena claims a planning stake rather than treating her help as borrowed time**

The Player thanks Rena and apologizes for involving her in setup meetings, but she answers that this is their event. Her reply gives a concrete ownership claim to work usually treated as backstage preparation, while the invitation to shop with him makes assistance reciprocal. Kyoko’s earlier wish initiates the beyond-live idea through a report; Rena’s contribution is to help give it practical form. Neither act alone authorizes a claim that all three members have already chosen every detail.

Casual-looking clothes are selected to support an invitation scenario, not because the public event has become the members’ actual private home. The Player explains that fans should feel invited to a LeSca-hosted party; Rena notices his taste and also supplies a practical reason in movement and mixed activities. What looks ordinary is therefore part of authored presentation and logistics. The scene does not make casual dress a guaranteed disclosure of an authentic private self or proof that performer/fan access has no boundary.

The latter are absent, and Honoka’s preference is Rena’s report rather than current consent. The Player’s instruction to discuss and decide together is a specific corrective step. It is useful evidence of a local choice norm, but the chapter does not show that discussion or prove a lasting institutional process, an agreed design or a resolved body-image concern.

#### Factual reconstruction

#### Interpretation and alternatives

**CL-B0251-064 — Excitement begins the work but does not decide its details**

Honoka’s initial enthusiasm for bingo produces a concrete task she cannot solve simply by wanting it. Budget, gift identity and seasonal appropriateness become practical constraints. The Player supports the impulse before asking what she had considered, allowing an incomplete plan to be developed rather than treating uncertainty as proof she should not have proposed it. The scene does not certify a complete budget or prove that all planning pressure has disappeared.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0218.json__98c5ab60270114cc?doc=primary&log=1#/Pages/1` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0218.json__98c5ab60270114cc?doc=primary&log=12#/Pages/12` (complete P1-12 context).

**CL-B0251-065 — The colleagues’ list is both useful and insufficient as a model of self**

The HoloCom list gives Honoka examples she regards as characteristic of Kyoko and Rena, but it does not yet tell her what to choose for herself. The Player’s Christmas-fit objections add another criterion beside personal signature. Because the source never maps each item to an author, assigning sword to one colleague or grip trainer to another from assumed personality would replace evidence with a stereotype. Honoka’s comparison is meaningful without that invented mapping.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0218.json__98c5ab60270114cc?doc=primary&log=13#/Pages/13` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0218.json__98c5ab60270114cc?doc=primary&log=25#/Pages/25` (complete P13-25 context).

**CL-B0251-066 — Closeness is offered as hospitality and then delimited**

Honoka explains the home-party concept through a wish for fans to feel nearer and reaches for homemade food as a personal offering. The Player invokes hygiene, then the seasonal atmosphere of the room, to limit the proposals. This is a concrete negotiation over what a public party can contain. His given reasons are not an independent safety finding or proof of Honoka’s cooking quality; neither the wish nor the veto establishes that every audience boundary is settled.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0218.json__98c5ab60270114cc?doc=primary&log=26#/Pages/26` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0218.json__98c5ab60270114cc?doc=primary&log=32#/Pages/32` (complete P26-32 context).

**CL-B0251-067 — Shopping has progressed, but the gift’s meaning remains open**

The source marks an actual narrative step beyond proposals: the Player reports checkout complete and Honoka reports purchases. Yet the purchased set stays unspecified, and the later hot-water-bottle thought is a new proposal. Keeping those stages apart permits real progress without prematurely awarding a future prize or importing a full receipt. The difference also matters to the family’s prospective sequence: later fulfillment must add evidence rather than rewrite this point.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0218.json__98c5ab60270114cc?doc=primary&log=35#/Pages/35` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0218.json__98c5ab60270114cc?doc=primary&log=42#/Pages/42` (complete P35-42 context); `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0218.json__98c5ab60270114cc?doc=primary&log=56#/Pages/56`; `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0218.json__98c5ab60270114cc?doc=primary&log=57#/Pages/57`.

**CL-B0251-068 — Honoka seeks a part in the work as well as its result**

When the Player offers to carry everything, Honoka insists on taking a bag because carrying presents itself feels exciting. Her reason gives practical participation an affective value; she is not merely being protected from all labor or waiting for the finished event. His acceptance is a local response to her request. It does not prove a complete equality of work or a physically watched handover, but it preserves her stated wish to participate.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0218.json__98c5ab60270114cc?doc=primary&log=38#/Pages/38` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0218.json__98c5ab60270114cc?doc=primary&log=42#/Pages/42` (complete P38-42 context).

**CL-B0251-069 — A family memory gives the public concept a personal aim**

The Player’s question shifts the choice problem from objects toward Honoka’s remembered feeling. Busy parents made celebration happen in another week, and she remembers food and togetherness warmly. Her desired gift to visitors becomes that felt warmth. The family memory supplies a situated source for hospitality rather than proof that every Christmas was identical, that busyness meant neglect, or that a paid public event becomes actual private-home access.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0218.json__98c5ab60270114cc?doc=primary&log=43#/Pages/43` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0218.json__98c5ab60270114cc?doc=primary&log=52#/Pages/52` (complete P43-52 context).

**CL-B0251-070 — Being herself includes a way of speaking rather than only a prize object**

Honoka’s dialect becomes visible as she articulates what she wants to give, then she corrects to a more formal wording. The Player accepts the original manner as characteristic of her, and she thanks him. This local affirmation gives the search for Honoka-like content a form in the conversation itself. It does not establish performed accent, a language policy, complete confidence or a permanent end to her earlier body and presentation anxieties.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0218.json__98c5ab60270114cc?doc=primary&log=52#/Pages/52` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0218.json__98c5ab60270114cc?doc=primary&log=55#/Pages/55` (complete P52-55 context).

**CL-B0251-071 — The final object joke leaves generosity concrete and unfinished**

Emotional warmth becomes a literal warm object in the hot-water-bottle proposal. The Player’s uncertain reply keeps the choice open, rather than explicitly approving or rejecting it. The joke does not erase Honoka’s clarified desire for the guests; it returns that desire to the practical question of what can be bought and offered. A metaphorical insight and an undecided material implementation coexist at the freeze.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0218.json__98c5ab60270114cc?doc=primary&log=51#/Pages/51` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0218.json__98c5ab60270114cc?doc=primary&log=58#/Pages/58` (complete P51-58 context).

**CL-B0251-072 — The device and mall do not replace reported knowledge with a visual audit**

The foreground supports the act of consulting a HoloCom, but its generic branding does not show the gift list or its authors. The mall backdrop and ordinary Honoka sprite also do not show a receipt, prize contents or the childhood party. Named speech remains the evidence for those parts of the account. This distinction preserves both actual static review and the source’s mediated family/colleague knowledge without inventing images or transactions.

#### Factual reconstruction

- **EV-B0251-055 / P0–P6:** A Chapter3 command page precedes Kyoko telling the Player that she enjoyed karaoke after a long interval. Asked why they went, she says it was to choose a cover song for the Christmas event. The Player's hesitation prompts her boast that he should appreciate exclusive access to her beautiful voice; he denies dissatisfaction and says she seemed to enjoy herself. The karaoke and its singing are discussed after the fact, not performed in these pages. `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0219.json__d3035f5a22169489?doc=primary&log=0#/Pages/0` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0219.json__d3035f5a22169489?doc=primary&log=6#/Pages/6` (complete P0-6 context).
- **EV-B0251-056 / P7–P15:** The Player says he was also made to sing; Kyoko explains it as the mood of the occasion. He praises her remembered choreography across singers and idols, including idols before the Seventh Sisters. Kyoko calls this diligent rival research, says she studied again after deciding to become an idol at Nanasta, and presents that work as normal for a professional and a leader. These are the speakers' accounts and appraisals, not an independently watched repertoire test. `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0219.json__d3035f5a22169489?doc=primary&log=7#/Pages/7` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0219.json__d3035f5a22169489?doc=primary&log=15#/Pages/15` (complete P7-15 context).
- **EV-B0251-057 / P16–P19:** Asked whether she found a song, Kyoko does not name a selection. She says solo singing would be easier, but she must consider Rena's and Honoka's keys; the short preparation period rules out excessive difficulty, and she insists on a Christmas feeling. The Player acknowledges that these constraints make the choice difficult. `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0219.json__d3035f5a22169489?doc=primary&log=16#/Pages/16` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0219.json__d3035f5a22169489?doc=primary&log=19#/Pages/19` (complete P16-19 context).

Asked whether she feels differently, Kyoko hesitantly says she also wants to be with someone she likes. Neither identifies that person's name. `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0219.json__d3035f5a22169489?doc=primary&log=28#/Pages/28` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0219.json__d3035f5a22169489?doc=primary&log=35#/Pages/35` (complete P28-35 context).

#### Interpretation and alternatives

**CL-B0251-073 — Pleasure and preparation occupy the same reported activity**

The initial disagreement concerns how to describe an outing that both enjoyed recreation and served professional selection. Kyoko protects the professional account with a boast about her voice, while the Player notices her pleasure and recalls his own involuntary participation. Her subsequent account of renewed research makes skill a practice following a decision to join Nanasta, rather than an unexplained innate possession. The Player's perfection language remains his praise, not the analyst's audited performance grade.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0219.json__d3035f5a22169489?doc=primary&log=1#/Pages/1` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0219.json__d3035f5a22169489?doc=primary&log=15#/Pages/15` (complete P1-15 context).

**CL-B0251-074 — Leadership becomes a problem of accommodation**

Kyoko's assertion of leadership acquires concrete content in the next problem: a song she could sing alone may not fit Rena and Honoka. Member-specific keys, limited preparation time and seasonal tone constrain one another. This is a small but specific instance of coordinating a trio rather than treating Kyoko's ability as the group's ability. The scene stops at deliberation, so leadership intention must not be promoted to a completed inclusive arrangement.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0219.json__d3035f5a22169489?doc=primary&log=15#/Pages/15` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0219.json__d3035f5a22169489?doc=primary&log=19#/Pages/19` (complete P15-19 context).

**CL-B0251-075 — A reference request creates room for a personal question**

Kyoko invites the Player's taste into a task whose main constraints she has already set. His repetition earns correction; his romantic/sweet/gentle answer supplies a new criterion and shifts the conversation's register. Her 'for reference' qualifier and surprise make the exchange readable as personally charged while leaving its formal purpose intact. The analyst need not choose between planning and attraction as exclusive explanations.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0219.json__d3035f5a22169489?doc=primary&log=20#/Pages/20` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0219.json__d3035f5a22169489?doc=primary&log=28#/Pages/28` (complete P20-28 context).

**CL-B0251-076 — The missing referent sustains apparent agreement**

The phrase about being with someone one likes contains an unfilled referent. Kyoko's startled response, hesitant reciprocal statement, direct address to the manager and brief assent invite an interpretation of personal hope. Yet neither speaker names the other as the loved person. The Player's claim of shared feeling therefore exceeds what the exchange has explicitly aligned. Preserve this structural ambiguity rather than converting suggestive address into a confirmed confession.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0219.json__d3035f5a22169489?doc=primary&log=29#/Pages/29` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0219.json__d3035f5a22169489?doc=primary&log=39#/Pages/39` (complete P29-39 context).

**CL-B0251-077 — The fan frame changes what shared romance means**

The Player's explanation makes the guests' love of Le☆S☆Ca the basis for a romantic event. The same vocabulary now connects performers and audience rather than certifying a couple. Kyoko's surprise and ensuing ellipsis make the mismatch legible without giving the analyst unrestricted access to her thought. The source does not show that the Player knowingly set a trap, nor that he understood her earlier implication and rejected her.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0219.json__d3035f5a22169489?doc=primary&log=40#/Pages/40` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0219.json__d3035f5a22169489?doc=primary&log=44#/Pages/44` (complete P40-44 context).

**CL-B0251-078 — The ending leaves knowledge unequal**

Kyoko's insult supplies an overt negative reaction; the Player's question shows that he has not supplied an explanation for it. Readers can connect that reaction to the preceding shift of frame, but the script does not show Kyoko explaining her feeling, the Player learning a romantic intention, or either repairing the exchange. The comic stop preserves an epistemic gap as well as an unresolved song choice.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0219.json__d3035f5a22169489?doc=primary&log=45#/Pages/45`; `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0219.json__d3035f5a22169489?doc=primary&log=46#/Pages/46`; `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0219.json__d3035f5a22169489?doc=primary&log=47#/Pages/47`.

**CL-B0251-079 — Three preparatory perspectives remain distinct**

In the already frozen preceding chapters, Rena developed the event's invitation and appearance logic while Honoka sought a personally meaningful gift. Kyoko now handles repertoire and the problem of speaking to the audience through a song. This sequence distributes planning across LeSca without making the Player's separate encounters a shared conversation. Her concern for both colleagues' keys is current evidence of group orientation; it is not evidence that they know the details of this private exchange.

**CL-B0251-080 — Written and static evidence support a bounded reading**

The written contrast between an intimate-sounding exchange and an explicit fan-facing explanation, together with face-asset changes and shake directives, supports an interpretation of romantic misunderstanding. The available 24 Kyoko dialogue WAVs plus one carrier have not been heard; no singing performance or runtime has been observed. #### Interpretation and alternatives

**CL-B0251-081 — A reported success opens another desire**

The event's completion is reported through enjoyment and Rena's observation of guests, rather than through a playable reconstruction of the performance. Kyoko immediately points toward another live; Honoka and Rena articulate their own appetite for it. Success therefore produces prospective agency, while the exact songs, clothes and gifts from the separate planning scenes stay unspecified. The reader should not fill those details just because the public event is now past.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0220.json__ebc1edb44d45b3f2?doc=primary&log=1#/Pages/1` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0220.json__ebc1edb44d45b3f2?doc=primary&log=7#/Pages/7` (complete P1-7 context).

**CL-B0251-082 — Peer correction preserves difficulty inside enjoyment**

Rena's teasing tests Honoka's sweeping positive account against remembered lesson difficulty. Kyoko offers a temporal reinterpretation: painful work may acquire enjoyable meaning in the end, rather than having been easy all along. Rena apologizes and Honoka accepts, so the disagreement becomes a concrete local repair. Kyoko's generalization about live performance remains her belief; the analyst need not adopt it as a guarantee that art redeems every cost.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0220.json__ebc1edb44d45b3f2?doc=primary&log=8#/Pages/8` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0220.json__ebc1edb44d45b3f2?doc=primary&log=14#/Pages/14` (complete P8-14 context).

**CL-B0251-083 — Gratitude keeps a role claim and a personal concession**

Rena and Honoka name enabling work and accommodation. Kyoko does not simply abandon her demand on the manager: she first locates the work in his role, then grants that he is doing it well. Rena converts that mixed evaluation into a proposal for reciprocal action. Their gratitude coexists with expectations of labor, rather than settling a comprehensive account of who funded or performed every task.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0220.json__ebc1edb44d45b3f2?doc=primary&log=15#/Pages/15` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0220.json__ebc1edb44d45b3f2?doc=primary&log=18#/Pages/18` (complete P15-18 context).

**CL-B0251-084 — Santa changes the direction of hospitality**

Earlier preparations aimed to offer fans a home-party feeling and warmth. Rena now proposes that the performers become gift-givers for the enabling manager. The illumination is a shared experience to be shown, not a material object whose ownership is transferred. Honoka's question and approval make the proposal intelligible as a present; no purchase, booking, exclusive access or delivery has yet been established.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0220.json__ebc1edb44d45b3f2?doc=primary&log=18#/Pages/18` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0220.json__ebc1edb44d45b3f2?doc=primary&log=27#/Pages/27` (complete P18-27 context).

**CL-B0251-085 — Romance is acknowledged without identifying a couple**

Unlike the preceding Kyoko/Player exchange, the trio explicitly recognizes the proposed outing's romantic quality: Kyoko names it, Rena values it, Honoka encourages her and Kyoko agrees. This supplies a forward group decision with a known description. It does not retroactively resolve the earlier unnamed beloved or turn the coming four-person outing into a confirmed exclusive date or shared confession.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0220.json__ebc1edb44d45b3f2?doc=primary&log=28#/Pages/28` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0220.json__ebc1edb44d45b3f2?doc=primary&log=31#/Pages/31` (complete P28-31 context).

**CL-B0251-086 — The invitation reverses an earlier knowledge arrangement**

During the individual preparation chapters, the Player helped interpret each member's proposals. Here the trio coordinates first and gives him only the practical invitation. He agrees under Kyoko's insistence but openly cannot see the full plan; Rena postpones explanation until the rendezvous. The scene produces an affectionate surprise through selective disclosure, without demonstrating informed acceptance of every implication or a completed gift reception.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0220.json__ebc1edb44d45b3f2?doc=primary&log=34#/Pages/34` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0220.json__ebc1edb44d45b3f2?doc=primary&log=40#/Pages/40` (complete P34-40 context); `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0220.json__ebc1edb44d45b3f2?doc=primary&log=49#/Pages/49` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0220.json__ebc1edb44d45b3f2?doc=primary&log=53#/Pages/53` (complete P49-53 context).

**CL-B0251-087 — Costume consent is specific and prospective**

Honoka originates this chapter's suggestion to wear the event costumes, Kyoko endorses it and asks Rena, and Rena agrees. That is a current three-member decision about an intended later outing. It cannot certify that all three approved the earlier, more revealing suggestion Rena made while the others were absent, because no design or full earlier discussion is supplied. Nor do the ordinary sprites become evidence that a change of clothes has happened.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0220.json__ebc1edb44d45b3f2?doc=primary&log=41#/Pages/41` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0220.json__ebc1edb44d45b3f2?doc=primary&log=48#/Pages/48` (complete P41-48 context).

**CL-B0251-088 — Deferred greeting gives the ending a future address**

Kyoko and Honoka reserve Merry Christmas for later, and Rena ends by demanding punctuality to a promise. The native ending then offers an unoccupied illuminated park. This image gives the invitation an anticipatory visual destination while the scene withholds the meeting and reception. '終わり' ends the document, not every planned action. Treating the outing as completed would erase the distinction that the ending organizes.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0220.json__ebc1edb44d45b3f2?doc=primary&log=49#/Pages/49` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0220.json__ebc1edb44d45b3f2?doc=primary&log=56#/Pages/56` (complete P49-56 context).

**CL-B0251-089 — Specific local repair does not erase the broader model**

Rena's apology, Honoka's acceptance and Kyoko's protective intervention are positive current relational evidence. Their force lies in those attributed acts, not in claiming that the trio has no future conflict, that lesson hardship was unreal or that earlier perfection/body-image difficulties were cured. The unresolved romantic mismatch from Chapter3 likewise remains at its genuine horizon even as Kyoko joins a new group plan.

Evidence: `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0220.json__ebc1edb44d45b3f2?doc=primary&log=8#/Pages/8` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0220.json__ebc1edb44d45b3f2?doc=primary&log=17#/Pages/17` (complete P8-17 context); `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0220.json__ebc1edb44d45b3f2?doc=primary&log=28#/Pages/28` through `t7s://c20260909-r484/v1/script/memory_0220.json__ebc1edb44d45b3f2?doc=primary&log=31#/Pages/31` (complete P28-31 context).

**CL-B0251-090 — A missing command cue limits performance claims rather than text**

The strict binder failed on precisely memory_0217_131 at a command-only carrier. A fresh full canonical-registry scan and the retained memory0220 bank confirm that this cue is unresolved there; all 46 dialogue cues and the title carrier do resolve. The native command, actor fades and subsequent still remain usable as native/static evidence, while no unheard speech, intentional silence, audible farewell or actual later arrival is invented. This qualification preserves the failed binding and the source, not a replacement performance.

### Complete-family factual reconstruction

- The four numbered chapters move from three separately focalized preparation conversations to a shared post-event conversation and a further invitation. They do not reproduce the event itself. Chapter1 has Rena and Player discussing a Christmas event whose venue/staff arrangements are reported, rather than a meeting or inspection performed on-page. Rena treats it as their event and develops a public home-party idea: fans should feel closer to the group, with casual clothing and varied activities. Her more revealing-costume suggestion concerns absent Kyoko/Honoka; Player calls for proper group discussion, and no design is accepted in that exchange.
- Rena's speculation about funding and gifts includes Player and the many staff who make idol work possible. This is an expressed gratitude and a proposal for reciprocal pleasure, not a verified purchase or complete financial account. Her Santa/reindeer teasing does not allocate binding roles or establish the clothes eventually worn.
- Chapter2 shifts to Honoka's difficulty choosing meaningful bingo prizes under a budget. She presents the combined Kyoko/Rena suggestions through HoloCom; the text does not assign the wooden sword, grip trainer or horror movie to an individual colleague. A wish to bring fans closer leads to proposed handmade sweets and ramen, which Player questions with stated hygiene and smell reasons. These exchanges do not certify actual food preparation, safety outcomes or her cooking skill.
- Chapter2 reports that checkout/purchases have finished, and Honoka asks to share the carrying because the paper bags make her excited. Exact purchased goods, prices and financing remain unnamed. Her memory of busy parents celebrating together a week before Christmas grounds the wish to give fans a warm feeling. The hot-water-bottle idea comes after checkout and receives Player's uncertain response; it is not an established purchase or awarded prize. Her shift from literal dialect プレゼントしたいたい to formal プレゼントしたいです remains an authored self-correction, not an extractor repair.
- Chapter3 gives Kyoko's account of choosing a Christmas cover through karaoke. Player reports being made to sing and praises her remembered choreography, including pre-Seventh performers. Kyoko says she studied again after deciding to become an idol at Nanasta and frames such work as professional and leaderly. Actual singing, choreography, heard delivery and a repertoire test are not present in the acquired scene.
- Kyoko's song choice must accommodate Rena's and Honoka's keys, a short preparation period and Christmas atmosphere. She asks Player's taste; he eventually proposes romantic, sweet and gentle music. Their talk about spending Christmas with someone one likes appears to align, but no loved person is named. When Player explicitly frames the romantic event around fans' love of LeSca, Kyoko reacts negatively and calls him stupid; he asks why she is angry. The song and their interpretive mismatch remain unresolved at the Chapter3 freeze.
- Chapter4 begins after the event in the speakers' accounts: Honoka enjoyed it, Rena reports smiling guests and Kyoko already wants another live. The chapter does not specify which proposed clothes, song or prizes were used. Rena's teasing about hard lessons challenges Honoka's sweeping positive account; Kyoko interprets Honoka's meaning as the capacity to turn difficulty into enjoyment. Rena explicitly apologizes to Honoka, who accepts. Kyoko then articulates a more general belief about the happiness of being able to perform.
- Rena and Honoka credit Player's enabling labor; Kyoko calls it his job while conceding good work. Rena originates a reciprocal illumination-present plan for the manager she describes as confined to Nanasta. Honoka welcomes it, Kyoko calls it romantic, Rena and Honoka encourage her, and Kyoko agrees. This is a specific current group decision, not evidence of an exclusive date or a resolution of the preceding private misunderstanding.
- The trio then invites Player at the office. Honoka checks his availability, Rena promises something good to see, and Kyoko presses for assent. Player agrees without knowing the full surprise. Rena sets 7109 Street at seven that evening. Honoka announces a plural return to the women's dorm, Rena her own return home; Rena explains they will change clothes. Honoka proposes the event costumes, Kyoko welcomes this and asks Rena, who accepts. These plans are distinct from actual return or change and from consent to Rena's earlier unspecified revealing-costume proposal.
- Player says he cannot follow the plan. Rena postpones explanation to the meeting place; Kyoko and Honoka reserve Merry Christmas for later, and Rena requests punctuality to a promise. The ending removes the actors, changes to an empty illuminated nighttime park and displays 終わり. It offers a visual horizon for the planned outing, but no text or character state supplies arrival, greeting, illumination viewing, gift reception or Player's response. ### Complete-family causal structure

- The whole family organizes reciprocal hospitality rather than a checklist of completed Christmas attractions. Rena asks how a public event can feel welcoming; Honoka asks what makes a gift carry personally known warmth; Kyoko asks how a group can address listeners through a feasible song. The public event is the shared practical problem, while each individual conversation supplies a different criterion. Their coexistence is established; a fully reconciled final design is not.
- Material limits are causal, not decorative. Staff arrangements, Nanasta funding proposals, a stated gift budget, hygiene/smell objections, individual keys and short preparation time prevent wishes from immediately becoming outcomes. Honoka's reported checkout is a genuine completed stage even though the goods remain unspecified. Retaining that stage alongside unresolved later proposals is more accurate than calling all preparation either successful or merely imagined.
- Rena's home-party comparison is an audience relation rather than literal private access to an idol's home. Honoka's family memory similarly transfers an intended feeling, not a documented replica of her household. The two passages make hospitality something deliberately composed for others. They do not erase boundaries between the public event, remembered family life and personal affection.
- Kyoko's chapter exposes the risk built into this language of closeness. A phrase about being with a liked person can sustain a private-sounding and a fan-facing frame until Player names the audience. The apparent agreement was underdetermined; its collapse supplies comedy and a continuing knowledge gap. This is not evidence that romantic art cannot be sincere or that Player consciously rejects a clearly named confession.
- The final chapter does not solve the public event by enumerating the chosen activities. Instead, reported audience pleasure permits the trio to ask what they want to give next. A question about another live briefly opens a continuing work cycle. The peer exchange about hard lessons prevents post-event pleasure from simply replacing the remembered labor that produced it.
- Kyoko's defense, Rena's apology and Honoka's acceptance constitute a concrete local repair. The positive result depends on acts by distinct members, rather than a generic claim that the unit is harmonious. Kyoko's statement that live performance can transform everything into enjoyment remains an attributed value claim that the analyst can examine without adopting as universal truth.
- The direction of the gift then reverses. The manager who helped the individual members plan becomes the invited recipient of the trio's shared experience. Rena initiates the reversal, Honoka welcomes it and Kyoko accepts a plan explicitly described as romantic. This shared description is clearer than the earlier private alignment, but it still does not specify a couple or retrospectively reveal the earlier unnamed beloved.
- Selective disclosure creates the surprise. The audience has already seen the trio's plan; Player receives an invitation and practical time/place but asks what they mean. His assent establishes willingness to go at that moment, not full knowledge of every implication. The performers temporarily become hosts, while his understanding and the intended reception remain future.
- The costume exchange gives a limited, forward instance of group agreement: Honoka proposes the current costumes, Kyoko checks with Rena and Rena agrees. It answers how they intend to present themselves at the outing. It does not repair an unseen earlier decision or authorize the analyst to invent a costume design, purchase, try-on or actual change.
- The ending's reserved greeting and unoccupied illuminated park keep the gift oriented toward a future encounter. A formal ending can close the narrative unit while leaving a promised action unperformed in the record. Erasing that limit would remove the family's distinction between enabling, proposing, agreeing, reporting and receiving—the very sequence through which its hospitality acquires meaning.

### Complete-family epistemic boundaries

- Chapter order is genuine consumption order: case1 then2 then3 then4, each frozen before the next primary exposure. Their numbering and final post-event account support a preparation-to-post-event structure. They do not establish exact dates for the three separate outings or a complete calendar relative to other Event families. Preserve catalog metadata pre-exposure and the difference between data availability and actual reading.
- Player participates in each individual preparation conversation. That does not mean Rena, Honoka and Kyoko possess the contents of one another's separate exchanges. The combined gift list gives no item-to-author map; the Player/Kyoko romantic mismatch is not relayed to the others. The final trio planning scene supplies common knowledge only for those shown taking part.
- Rena reports the guests' smiles and describes Player's confinement to Nanasta. Kyoko reports her research and supplies the interpretation of Honoka's enjoyment. Honoka recalls her own family warmth. Each remains a speaker's access and framing, not an omniscient audit, a watched flashback or a complete psychological explanation.
- Only Chapter4 promotes the Christmas event itself to past in the speakers' account. Earlier song, costume and prize choices stay unresolved at their genuine case freezes and remain unspecified in the final text. Do not backfill them from a generic successful-event statement.
- Chapter3's liked-person referents are unnamed. The suggestive address and reactions can support a qualified misunderstanding/attraction reading, not a definitive mutual confession or demonstrated Player knowledge of Kyoko's implication. The final group plan's explicit romantic description is new local evidence, not a retrospective solution.
- The promised illumination outing, meeting, return home/dorm, change of clothes and reserved greeting remain future at the last written page. The final textless park is a reader-visible still, with no actors or witnessed receipt. Do not convert its presence into character arrival or assign an independently unproven location identity.

The original strict failure, fresh probe, case-only successor and18negative controls are preserved. ### Complete-family character relations

- Rena's agency spans invitation design, recognition of staff support, a later apology, initiation of the reciprocal present and concrete rendezvous logistics. Her informal teasing is not flattened into cruelty or complete selflessness; the apology is an explicit change in the current exchange. Her earlier absent-member costume proposal remains distinct from the later trio decision.
- Honoka makes her own contribution through family memory and the search for warmth, not simply by accepting Rena's or Kyoko's ideas. The reported purchases and shared carrying request supply practical initiative. In the final chapter she accepts the apology, welcomes the gift, asks availability, proposes the costumes and defers the greeting. This does not cure earlier body surveillance or establish consent to an unspecified earlier design.
- Kyoko makes professional identity concrete through renewed study and group-specific song constraints. Her possible personal hope complicates audience-facing romance without identifying a confirmed partner. Her later defense of Honoka, mixed role-demand/praise toward Player, explicit romantic concern and agreement to the invitation show different relational actions that must remain attributed and locally timed.
- Player is an enabling organizer and interlocutor with his own taste, misunderstandings and limited information. Rena's first chapter recognizes many staff, so final thanks do not prove that he alone produced the event. His criticism of proposals remains attributed; his assent to an unexplained surprise does not show complete knowledge, a completed purchase, exclusive romance or actual gift reception.
- LeSca's cohesion is enacted through accommodation, disagreement, apology, encouragement and agreement, rather than inferred from the fact that the unit appears together. The family adds no member, new unit identity, principal-character readiness promotion or mature-monograph claim. Current source-local acts do not rewrite prior stable histories.
- The unnamed parents, guests and staff stay role references rather than new discovered individuals. Honoka's plural dorm-return plan is preserved without inventing a residence roster. Santa is a gift-giving metaphor here, not a new participant or costume shown in the current portraits.

### Complete-family literary synthesis and alternatives

**CL-B0251-091 — Hospitality is composed through different practical questions**

Rena develops the public invitation, Honoka seeks a gift that carries remembered warmth, and Kyoko considers feasible group song selection. Budget, stated hygiene/smell concerns, individual keys and limited time resist immediate realization. This is distributed planning, not proof that every proposal was implemented or a literal opening of an idol's home to fans.
